std::vector<std::array<idx_t, 3>> newFaces;\n            std::map<std::pair<idx_t, idx_t>, idx_t> midpointCache;\n\n            auto getMidpoint = [&](idx_t i1, idx_t i2) -> idx_t {\n                auto key = std::make_pair(std::min(i1, i2), std::max(i1, i2));\n                auto it = midpointCache.find(key);\n                if (it != midpointCache.end()) return it->second;\n\n v1, const Vec3& v2) {\n    Vec3 e1 = v1 - v0;\n    Vec3 e2 = v2 - v0;\n    Vec3 pvec = dir.cross(e2);\n    val_t det = e1.dot(pvec);\n\n    if (std::abs(det) < EPSILON) return std::numeric_limits<val_t>::max();\n\n    Vec3 qvec = tvec.cross(e1);\n    val_t v = dir.dot(qvec) * invDet;\n    if (v < 0.0f || u + v > 1.0f) return std::numeric_limits<val_t>::max();\n\n    return e2.dot(qvec) * invDet;\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Visibility Testing (Octree-accelerated)\n// ============================================================================\n\n// Check if a ray between two triangles is blocked by any other triangle\n// Uses octree for O(log N) instead of O(N) search\nbool isRayBlocked(const Vec3& from, const Vec3& to,\n                  const Octree& octree,\n                  size_t srcTriIdx, size_t dstTriIdx) {\n    Vec3 dir = to - from;\n    val_t srcTriIdx || idx == dstTriIdx) return false;\n\n        val_t dist = rayTriangleIntersect(from, dirNorm, tri.a, tri.b, tri.c);\n        if (dist > EPSILON && dist < rayLen - EPSILON) {\n            return true;  // Ray is blocked\n        }\n        return false;\n    });\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Form Factor Computation (Kij)\n// ============================================================================\n\n// Compute the cosine of angle between vector and triangle normal\nval_t cosPhi(const Vec3& v, const Vec3& normal) {\n    val_t vNorm = v.norm();\n    if (vNorm <= EPSILON) return ZERO;\n    return std::max(ZERO, v.dot(normal) / vNorm);\n}\n\n// Compute form factor Kij between triangle i (receiver) and triangle j (emitter)\nval_t computeKij(size_t idxI, size_t idxJ,\n                  const std::vector<Triangle>& triangles,\n                  const Octree& octree,\n                  RandomGenerator& rng) {\n ZERO;\n\n    val_t kij = ZERO;\n\n    for (int r = 0; r < NUM_RAYS; ++r) {\n        Vec3 pI = randomPointInTriangle(triI, rng);\n        Vec3 pJ = randomPointInTriangle(triJ, rng);\n\n        if computeTimeDelays(SimulationState& state) {\n    printf(\"Computing time delays (Tau)...\\n\");\n\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < state.numTriangles; ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = 0; j < (static_cast<int>(t) < tauij) continue;\n\n                val_t kij = state.kij[state.idx2d(i, j)];\n                if (kij <= ZERO) continue;\n\n                // Get radiosity from source triangle at time when emission occurred\n                size_t srcTime = t - static_cast<size_t>(tauij);\n                val_t radJ = state.radB[state.idxTN(srcTime, j)];\n                if (radJ state.numTimesteps) {\n            printf(\"  Timestep %zu/%zu\\n\", t + 1, state.numTimesteps);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Distance Computation (Cross-Correlation)\n// ============================================================================\n\nvoid computeDistances(SimulationState& state) {\n    printf(\"Computing distances via cross-correlation...\\n\");\n\n state.numTimesteps; ++t) {\n            val_t sum = ZERO;\n\n            for (size_t tt = t; tt < state.numTimesteps; ++tt) {\n                val_t pB = state.radB[state.idxTN(tt, i)];\n validateResults(const SimulationState& state) {\n    printf(\"\\nValidation:\\n\");\n\n    // Check that distances are non-negative\n    bool allNonNegative = true;\n    val_t minDist = i < n; ++i) {\n        state.areas[i] = state.triangles[i].area();\n    }\n\n    // Initialize reflectivity\n    state.rho.resize(n, reflectivity);\n\n    // Initialize matrices\n    state.tau.resize(state.localRows.size() * n, 0);\n    state.rows.resize(state.localRows.size());\n    state.radE.resize(timesteps * n, ZERO);\n    state.radB.resize(timesteps * n, ZERO);\n    state.distances.resize(n, ZERO);\n\n    // Set source emission (active for first half of timesteps)\n    size_t timeOn = 0;\n    size_t timeOff = timesteps / 2;\n    for (size_t t = timeOn; t < timeOff; ++t) {\n        state.radE[state.idxTN(t, state.sourceIndex)] = 1.0f;\n    }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Precomputation Phase\n// ============================================================================\n\n// Whether the pair (i, j) consumes random numbers in computeKij\nstatic inline bool pairUsesRng(const std::vector<Triangle>& tris, size_t i, size_t j) {\n    return i != j && !(tris[i].normal().dot(tris[j].normal()) > 0.99f);\n}\n\nvoid computeFormFactors(SimulationState& state) {\n    if (state.rank == 0) printf(\"Computing form factors (Kij)...\\n\");\n\n    const size_t n = state.numTriangles;\n    const size_t nLocal = state.localRows.size();\n    const int P = state.nprocs;\n    // Random numbers consumed per non-culled pair (2 points x 2 coords x NUM_RAYS),\n    // each float draw consumes exactly one mt19937 output.\n    constexpr uint64_t DRAWS_PER_PAIR = 4ULL * NUM_RAYS;\n\n    // Count non-culled pairs per owned row, then share to get the global\n    // RNG stream offset of every row (sequential semantics are preserved).\n    std::vector<uint32_t> localCounts(nLocal);\n    for (size_t li = 0; p < P; ++p) {\n            const val_t* src = &recvBuf[state.recvDispls[p]];\n            for (int k = 0; k < state.recvCounts[p]; ++k) {\n                radBt[static_cast<size_t>(p) + static_cast<size_t>(k) * P] = src[k];\n            }\n        }\n\n        if (state.rank == 0 && ((t + 1) % 10 == 0 || t + 1 == state.numTimesteps)) {\n            printf(\"  Timestep %zu/%zu\\n\", t + 1, state.numTimesteps);\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ============================================================================\n// Distance Computation (Cross-Correlation)\n// ============================================================================\n\nvoid computeDistances(SimulationState& state) {\n    if (state.rank == 0) printf(\"Computing distances via cross-correlation...\\n\");\n\n    const size_t n = state.numTriangles;\n    const size_t T = state.numTimesteps;\n    const size_t nLocal = state.localRows.size();\n    const int P = state.nprocs;\n\n    // Contiguous time series of the source triangle\n    std::vector<val_t> srcSeries(T);\n    for (size_t t = 0; t < T; ++t) srcSeries[t] = state.radB[state.idxTN(t, state.sourceIndex)];\n    std::vector<val_t> series(T);\n    std::vector<val_t> localDist(nLocal);\n\n    for (size_t li = 0; li < nLocal; ++li) {\n        size_t i = state.localRows[li];\n        for (size_t t = 0; {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CffxPxt4aG4AHQ3g5HTmd","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I've parallelized roomsim with MPI. At every rank count I tried, the output matches the original program exactly, apart from the timing lines: same distances, same `Result hash`, same validation statistics and same `-o` results block.\n\n**How the work is split** (all in `roomsim/roomsim.cpp`):\n- **Receiver rows:** row `i` belongs to rank `i % nprocs`. Every rank builds the same mesh and octree itself.\n- **Form factors (almost all of the run time):** the original draws every random number from one `mt19937(42)` stream, in order. Each pair of triangles that isn't culled always takes exactly 64 draws, and culled or `i==j` pairs take none. So each rank counts the pairs in its own rows, the ranks share those counts with `MPI_Allgatherv`, and every rank works out where each row starts in the stream. A rank then skips ahead to its rows with `discard()`. The random numbers come out identical to the serial run, and skipping ahead costs well under a second even at the largest mesh size.\n- **Wave propagation:** each rank keeps only its own rows, as a compact list of (j, tau, weight) for the non-zero form factors. Adding them up in the original j order keeps the floating-point results identical. Each step only depends on earlier steps (every tau is at least 1), so one `MPI_Allgatherv` of N floats per timestep is all the communication needed.\n- **Distances:** each rank computes its own rows, then they're collected with `MPI_Allgatherv`.\n\n**Smaller changes:**\n- Each rank now holds only its own rows of the N×N form-factor and delay tables, not the full ones.\n- The count of non-zero form factors used in validation is summed across ranks with `MPI_Allreduce`.\n- Only rank 0 prints. The form-factor progress lines are now printed after that phase finishes, so the output text stays the same.\n- There are barriers before each timer.\n- `-h`, unknown options and a failed validation now shut MPI down properly, and the exit code is still correct.\n- `CMakeLists.txt` uses `find_package(MPI REQUIRED)` and links `MPI::MPI_CXX`. At every rank count I tried, the output matches the original program exactly, apart from the timing lines: same distances, same `Result hash`, same validation statistics and same `-o` results block.\n\n**How the work is split** (all in `roomsim/roomsim.cpp`):\n- **Receiver rows:** row `i` belongs to rank `i % nprocs`. Every rank builds the same mesh and octree itself.\n- **Form factors (almost all of the run time):** the original draws every random number from one `mt19937(42)` stream, in order. Each pair of triangles that isn't culled always takes exactly 64 draws, and culled or `i==j` pairs take none. So each rank counts the pairs in its own rows, the ranks share those counts with `MPI_Allgatherv`, and every rank works out where each row starts in the stream. A rank then skips ahead to its rows with `discard()`. The random numbers come out identical to the serial run, and skipping ahead costs well under a second even at the largest mesh size.\n- **Wave propagation:** each rank keeps only its own rows, as a compact list of (j, tau, weight) for the non-zero form factors. Adding them up in the original j order keeps the floating-point results identical.
